**A5's §3d reasoning is untouched and remains the controlling constraint:** `authenticated` holds a **table-level** UPDATE on `pfin.account` (`003`, no column list) and `pfin` is Data-API-exposed, so **a direct PATCH reaches `closed_at` whatever functions exist.** The gate must therefore stay a trigger, and **anything moved out of the triggers and into this function stops being enforced.** The RPC is a **caller**, not a gate. This is recorded in the migration header as a prohibition, not only here.

> **Method note, and it is A5's own lesson pointed back at A5.** A5 warned: *"check an options set for containment before choosing within it."* The containment check was run **within** the options set and not **around** it — every option considered was a way to satisfy the trigger's requirement, and none asked whether the app could reach any of them. **A dominated option corrupts the record of what was considered; an unasked question leaves no record at all.**

#### B3 — Decision: two `SECURITY INVOKER` write-composition RPCs (F/CTO-ratified)

```
pfin.fn_close_account(p_account_id bigint, p_reason_code text,
                      p_closed_at timestamptz default now())  returns timestamptz
pfin.fn_reopen_account(p_account_id bigint,
                       p_effective_date date default null)     returns void
```

Each sets its GUC via `set_config(…, is_local => true)` — **the PL/pgSQL form of `set local`; plain `SET LOCAL <name> = <variable>` is not available in PL/pgSQL, which is a second reason the app could not do this inline** — then performs the single-row gated UPDATE **in the same transaction**. Pattern precedent, both INVOKER write-composition RPCs: `013 fn_create_manual_account` ([ADR-026](#adr-026)) and `042 fn_land_linked_accounts` ([ADR-037](#adr-037)).

**Considered and not chosen**, recorded so the set is not re-derived:

- **A PostgREST `request.headers` GUC** (carry the reason as an HTTP header, read it in the trigger). Avoids a new object, but **changes `058`'s audit writer** to take a second reason source; couples a fence to a vendor-specific GUC name; puts the carrier **outside the function contract**; and a header is set for the **whole request**, so an unrelated `pfin.account` PATCH in it carries a reason it has no business carrying.
- **A direct Postgres connection from the SvelteKit server action.** The only option that generalises to further same-transaction-GUC paths, and the reason it is named rather than dismissed — but it introduces a **second database transport** for the web app against [ADR-023](#adr-023)'s Data-API role-of-record posture and SECURITY §4.1, plus RLS-bearing connection wiring the web app does not have (`TenantBoundConnection` is Lock 13 mod #3, Python/worker-side). **Correct answer if a third GUC-carrying write path appears; disproportionate for one control.**

**Both functions ship together, not close-only.** Reopen works over a bare PATCH today, so close-only was the smaller change — and it would have left **two mechanisms writing one column** and no way to record a reopen date if one is ever wanted.

**Sub-decisions carried in the DDL rather than only here**, each because the opposite is what a later reader reaches for:

1. **The RPC does not set `pfin.actor`.** Inert today (a session always has `auth.uid()`, so the writer never reaches the GUC branch) and a **forgery channel** the moment A5's precedence were reordered. The one thing this function must not do is pre-load the value a reordering would start trusting.
2. **The RPC does not re-validate the `reason_code` vocabulary.** `057`'s CHECK is the single enforcement point; a NULL/empty argument is passed through as `''` so the **writer's own raise fires, naming the remedy**. A copy here would be a **third** representation of a closed vocabulary (CHECK / GUC / client picker) — and the client picker is already the third. **The client constant must be pinned by a test against `pg_constraint`, not by a comment** (the §7.6 *S1* shape: an assertion, not a comment).
3. **⚠ It does NOT enable a future column-level `revoke update (closed_at)`.** Stated because it is the obvious next inference and it is **false**: an INVOKER function runs with the **caller's** privileges and would lose the same grant, leaving no path at all. That hardening requires DEFINER, i.e. allowlist **4 → 5** and its own ratify. **Nobody should build a Lock-3-mod-1 column-restriction plan on this believing the groundwork is done.**
4. **Narrow by construction** (`and closed_at is null` / `is not null`). A double-submitted close is refused legibly instead of silently re-dating a closure. **This is not a fence on re-dating — see B4.**
5. `p_closed_at` defaults to **server `now()`**. The prior app path sent a **client** clock into a column the gate bounds against server `now()`, so a fast client drew a spurious *future-`closed_at`* refusal.
6. The refusal prefix is **`close refused:`**, deliberately distinct from the gate's **`account closure blocked`**, so the app can distinguish *a named-leg refusal* from *nothing to close* — and the message **does not discriminate** absent / not-yours / already-closed, which are one condition under RLS and whose separation would leak existence.

#### B4 — `closed_at` is IMMUTABLE on an already-closed account (**RULED** — F/CTO, 2026-08-03)

**The finding.** The gate fires on `old.closed_at is null and new.closed_at is not null` — the into-closed transition only, per Decision 3's own wording. So a **closed** account's `closed_at` could be **moved to another past date by a direct PATCH** under `003`'s table-level UPDATE grant, **without the three legs re-firing**. Moved *earlier*, to a date when the account still held value, it **falsifies the standing invariant** and — after `059`'s as-of re-point — **silently restates historical NAV**. Same class as the Decision-4 `currency` conjunct, one column over. Not introduced by this amendment; found while adding a caller to the gate.

**⚠ AUDITED BUT UNPREVENTED — which is why this is a HARDENING and not an incident.** The `(3b)` writer fires on `closed_at IS DISTINCT FROM` in **both** directions, so **every re-date, including any that occurred before this fence existed, wrote a second `closed` row into `pfin.account_event` and is recoverable from it.** The defect was never *detectability*; it was that the move was **recorded and not refused**. Recorded explicitly because the two are routinely conflated, and because it is what makes the disposition a fence rather than a remediation: **there is no lost population to reconstruct.**

**Decision — a second conjunct on the existing gate**, not a new trigger and not a new concept. Grounds: **Decision 3 already ratifies the correction path this forces** — *"corrections go through reopen → edit → re-close, which re-proves the invariant rather than assuming it survived."* The fence therefore adds enforcement without adding vocabulary, and the correction route it pushes users onto **re-proves all three legs** instead of assuming they survived. *(Rejected alternative: widen the gate's own condition to `new.closed_at is not null and new.closed_at is distinct from old.closed_at`. Behaviourally close, but it folds a distinct rule into the gate's entry condition, where the next reader would have to derive the immutability rule from a boolean rather than read it.)*

**Two scope statements, in the DDL as prohibitions rather than descriptions** (F/CTO instruction):

1. **It fences the DATE MOVE, not reopen.** `new.closed_at is not null` is **load-bearing, not a tidiness guard**. Drop it and `closed_at = null` starts raising — **blocking the very reopen → correct → re-close path that is this fence's own justification.** *A fence that blocks its own remedy is self-defeating:* it would leave a mis-dated closure **permanently uncorrectable**, which is strictly worse than the mutability it set out to remove.
2. **What it does NOT cover, so it is not read as total.** It does **not** constrain a re-close to a *different* date after a legitimate reopen — that path re-enters the gate (`old.closed_at is null`) and **re-proves all three legs at the new date**, which is the entire point: *the date may move, but only through a transition that re-validates it.* Nor does it reach `closed_at` on an **open** account; that is a closure, and the gate governs it.

**⚑ Consequence for QA, stated here so a designed red is not read as a regression.** The gate now has **seven** `raise` sites, not six. The battery's **(B5)** counts them from `pg_get_functiondef` and **will go red until re-asserted** — measured, not predicted: the amended function reports **7** under (B5)'s own counting expression. (B5)'s own text prescribes the response: *"a red here means a raise was added or removed: go read it, then assert it."*

**Verified behaviourally, not just by compilation** (live DB, rolled-back transaction, scratch shape): `closed → earlier date` **REFUSED**; `closed → NULL` (reopen) **PERMITTED** — the self-defeating failure mode, checked directly rather than reasoned about; `closed → same date` **PERMITTED** (`is distinct from` is false, so a no-op write is not an error); `open → closed` **PERMITTED** (the conjunct is silent; the gate governs).

#### B5 — Build-sequence correction (the A3 shape, applied to this amendment)

**`058`'s deliverables list gains item (7):** *+ `fn_close_account` and `fn_reopen_account`, the application's path to the gate and the audit writer.* The file now enumerates its own deliverables in its header for exactly A2's reason. **Section (7) is appended and (6) was not renumbered** — a section number already cited in review threads and a battery is a citation that silently rots if it moves.

**Placement — `058` itself, not a new migration file, and the reason is evidence rather than convenience.** The initial instinct was a separate file so the RPCs could carry their own POSTURE RATIONALE + CONTRACT block. That fails on inspection: **`058` already contains §(5), a re-authoring of `fn_land_linked_accounts` — a caller, not a fence** — so the header's *"EVERYTHING IN THIS FILE IS ONE UNIT"* is already broader than column-plus-fences. A separate file additionally collides on numbering (`059` is claimed) and would force a renumber across a worktree and a battery filename.

> **The same-file SAFETY argument does NOT extend here, and this is recorded so the precedent is not copied without its condition.** `058`'s same-file rule exists because a column must never exist ungated. **A missing close RPC fails closed** — nobody can close anything — so a separate file would have been *safe*, merely worse on the two grounds above. **Same placement, different reason.**

#### B6 — Capability-verify (run, not assumed)

Per the standing rule that an ADR-named DB primitive is an **unrun assumption** until exercised, three propositions were verified against a live database in a **rolled-back transaction** (no `db reset`; the local `pfin.account` has no `closed_at`, so a scratch shape reproducing the trigger/GUC interaction was used):

1. **`set_config(…, true)` inside a function body IS visible to a trigger fired by that same body's UPDATE** — the mechanism the entire design rests on. ✅
2. **`UPDATE … RETURNING … INTO` sets `FOUND = false` on a zero-row match**, so the narrow-by-construction refusal actually fires rather than returning NULL silently. ✅
3. **The GUC does not leak**: a subsequent bare UPDATE in the same session still raises, so the RPC does not weaken the writer's requirement for any other write path. ✅

Also confirmed from `pg_proc`: both functions are **`prosecdef = false`** (INVOKER) with **`search_path=""`**. **Still unverified and named as such:** the end-to-end path through PostgREST against a database with `058` applied — that needs `057`/`058` applied locally, which needs a reset, which destroys live local test data. **Backend's first exercise of the control is the discharge point.**

#### B7 — Ledger impacts

- **DEFINER allowlist stays 4 (3 authored)** — both functions are `SECURITY INVOKER`. Any counter-proposal for DEFINER is **4 → 5** and a separate F/CTO ratify.
- **Decision-3 family — THESE TWO FUNCTIONS add no instance** (neither has an FK-shaped column). **⚠ Scoped deliberately: this is NOT a claim that the PR leaves the family flat. It does not — `057` grows it by one (#16).** The earlier wording here read *"Decision-3 family unchanged"*, which is true of the functions and false of the PR, and it was **relayed onward in the second sense** into a Sec brief. *(Read the count live from Decision 3.)*
- **§10 catalogued ledger unchanged** — the RPCs are **Lock 14 user-facing-direct-DB-write CLASS members, and class membership is not a catalogued instance**, the identical ruling this ADR's Consequences already made for the `058` fences. 3-axis clean: numbering unchanged, no layer-attribution move, no surface becomes four-layer; Decision 4 is referenced, not restated.
- **ADR-011 Decision 1 does NOT apply** — recorded explicitly so it is not re-adjudicated: `057`'s POSTURE RATIONALE and Decision 5a both rule the user's close/reopen *a session write under RLS, not a Decision 1 privileged-context surface*. **D2 applies** (the path causes an immutable audit-class INSERT).
- **Clause (d) is NOT discharged.** The general same-transaction audit-log helper is still unauthored (SELF-201 Task #7); both functions carry the `042`-shape forward-hook. **`pfin.account_event` does not discharge it** — that table records the **state transition**, the general log records the **action on this write path**, and reading the first as satisfying the second is how clause (d) gets quietly marked done.

**Sec joint-review triggers (enumerated for the review request):** money-adjacent write path · **Lock 14** user-facing direct-DB-write surface · **ADR-011 D2** · the **B4 open finding** on `closed_at` mutability · the refusal-message posture (whether raw gate text passes to the user, which names internal function identifiers, or is mapped to copy — Decision 1 requires the blocking **leg** be named either way).

**One-way doors.** The DDL is **not** one — an additive INVOKER RPC is droppable. **This amendment is**, in the sense that matters: absent it, a ratified ADR records `fn_close_account` as rejected while one ships, and the next reader has no way to tell which is current. The **PostgREST signature** is weakly one-way — named-argument dispatch makes a later **defaulted** parameter backward-compatible, while a rename or removal breaks clients.

### Amendment 3 (2026-08-04) — Sec RED on PR #318: the audit surface was writable by its own tenants

**Status: RATIFIED** — F/CTO, 2026-08-04 (F2 option **B**). Sec joint-review returned **RED with one veto**; this records the resolution.

#### C1 — F1, the veto: `actor = 'system:remediation'` was forgeable by any authenticated tenant

Four legs, each independently true and jointly fatal: `pfin` is Data-API-exposed; `057` grants `select, insert` on `account_event` to `authenticated`; **the `account_event_insert` WITH CHECK mentioned `actor` zero times**; and the `actor` CHECK admits `'system:remediation'` **unconditionally**. So a tenant POSTs `{users_id: <self>, account_id: <own>, actor: 'system:remediation'}` — **#16 passes** (the pair genuinely matches), the tenant conjunct passes (it genuinely is their uid), the CHECK passes (the value is genuinely in the vocabulary) — and a forged system-attributed row lands **permanently** in an append-only table with **no redaction path for anyone, including its own tenant**.

> **Why this is a veto and not a flag, and it is the most useful sentence in this amendment.** `058`'s single longest passage argues that this exact forgery is *"unreachable rather than merely discouraged"*. **That argument is true about the trigger and was defeated by a route that never enters it.** The passage reasons carefully about `auth.uid()`-before-GUC precedence — a real property, correctly derived — while the table sat directly writable over PostgREST. ***A reachability argument must be made over every path to the surface, not over the path being guarded.*** The more rigorous the local argument, the more completely it substitutes for the global one.

**Fix:** `account_event_insert`'s WITH CHECK gains `actor = 'user:' || auth.uid()::text`. Compatibility holds three ways, verified rather than asserted: the writer is INVOKER and emits exactly that string on its `auth.uid()` branch; its GUC branch is reachable only when `auth.uid()` is null, which cannot occur under a `to authenticated` policy; and the migration-role remediation writer is RLS-exempt, so no policy applies to it. **The conjunct is not redundant with the `actor` CHECK — the CHECK constrains SHAPE, the conjunct binds VALUE TO IDENTITY**, and only the second closes the hole. Recorded in the policy comment because "redundant with the CHECK" is exactly how it would be removed.

#### C2 — F2: fabricated-but-well-formed rows, and the precedent that was inverted silently

F1 closes **who a row claims to be**. It does not close **whether the event happened**: a tenant could still POST correctly-attributed, correctly-tenanted `closed`/`reopened` rows for their own accounts describing transitions that never occurred.

**Sec's finding is not "DEFINER is required." It is that [ADR-011](#adr-011) Decision 9's ratified precedent was inverted without being named.** D9 says, verbatim, on the structurally identical reclass-history surface:

> *"an INVOKER+grant path would let a user POST forged history rows — defeating the tamper-evidence."*

`grant insert … to authenticated` on an append-only audit table **is** that path. **The precedent was not argued against; it was not noticed.** ⚑ *A ratified precedent inverted silently is worse than one overruled loudly — an overruled precedent leaves an argument a reviewer can check, and a silent inversion leaves the reviewer re-deriving from scratch, which is what did not happen.*

**Decision — option B (F/CTO, 2026-08-04): an origin fence admitting only trigger-originated inserts, with a ROLE-based exemption for the migration-role remediation. Allowlist stays 4.**

| | **A** — DEFINER writer + no INSERT grant (D9's own remedy) | **B** — origin fence, INVOKER (**ratified**) |
|---|---|---|
| strength | stronger: authenticates the **caller** | authenticates the **call path** |
| DEFINER allowlist | **4 → 5** — puts the closure path in the elevated set | **stays 4** |
| RLS | writer runs elevated; #16 + the tenant + aal2 conjuncts stop binding on it | every write still evaluates under the caller's RLS — **all three keep binding** |
| residual | — | rests on there being exactly **one** trigger on `pfin.account` inserting here; a second needs a migration, therefore a review |

**B's one weakness is stated in the DDL rather than left to be discovered**, per the standing rule that a fence names what makes it necessary *and* what it rests on.

> **⚠ Sec's ratify condition, and it is the load-bearing half: THE EXEMPTION MUST NOT BE GUC-KEYED.** A `current_setting('pfin.remediation')`-style exemption would **reintroduce F1's entire class one fence over** — `set local` is available to any session, so the exemption would be **self-issuable** and the fence would be checking a claim the attacker writes. Implemented as **ownership of `pfin.account_event`** rather than a hardcoded role name: it names the *property* instead of the *identity*, so it does not rot when the role is renamed or differs across local / CI / production, and **ownership is not self-issuable**.

**`pg_trigger_depth()` measured, not assumed** (live DB, rolled-back transaction; the authenticated path reproduced with `set local role authenticated`, which is what PostgREST does — authenticator connection plus `SET LOCAL ROLE` — so nothing about the transport adds nesting): a direct INSERT makes the fence see **1**; an INSERT issued from inside `account_event_write` makes it see **2**. Hence `< 2` refuses.

> **⚠ BOTH NAIVE FORMS FAIL OPEN, which is why the numbers are in the DDL rather than the reasoning.** `pg_trigger_depth() > 0` — the form anyone would reach for to mean *"came from a trigger"* — **admits every write this fence exists to refuse**, because a direct insert presents **1, not 0**: the fence is itself a trigger. `= 0` refuses nothing, for the same reason. Under either, the legitimate path keeps working and nothing looks wrong. ***A silently inert fence is worse than no fence, because it is claimed.*** Re-measure before changing the predicate; do not derive it.

**The trigger NAME is ordering, not labelling — and this reverses my first implementation.** Triggers on one event fire **alphabetically**. I first named it `account_event_origin_fence`, which sorts *after* `account_event_matched_account`, so **#16 fired first** — meaning a direct POST was diagnosed as a *cross-tenant* defect when it is really a *wrong-origin* one, and the origin fence was reached only when the pair happened to match. Renamed to **`account_event_block_direct_insert`**, which sorts first and also joins the existing `_block_mutation` / `_block_truncate` family. **Ordering the DDL around a test's convenience would be backwards; the expectations get renamed, not the trigger.**

> **DESIGNED RED, PRE-ANNOUNCED (the Amendment 2 `(B5)` precedent).** `057`'s **(D1c)** and **(D2a)** are authenticated-tier and now raise the direct-insert fence instead of the `#16` pattern. **That red is this fence, not a regression in `#16`.** (D2b)/(D2c) are migration-role, exempt, unaffected. *(This supersedes my earlier report that nothing QA had written would go red from F2 — true of the first naming, false of the ratified one.)*

**And the rename SHARPENS `#16`'s justification rather than weakening it.** With the direct-insert fence firing first, what still reaches `#16` is (a) trigger-originated inserts, where the writer derives `users_id` from the account row and is therefore always matched, and (b) **the migration-role remediation**, which the direct-insert fence exempts by ownership and which is RLS-exempt. So `#16`'s live blast radius is now almost exactly the writer Decision 5a justified it by. **⚠ Do not read *"#16 rarely fires"* as *"#16 is redundant"*** — see C5.

#### C3 — F3: #16 folded into Decision 3's canonical enumeration

Done at [Decision 3](#adr-011) — sixteen labeled (#1–#16), thirteen DDL-realized, with the fold-in resolution recording that **the #15 slip recurred verbatim** and the rule that prevents a third: **the fold-in is due in the PR that DDL-realizes the instance.** The sharpest consequence, which is what makes it more than untidiness: a reviewer obeying the standing *read-Decision-3-live* discipline finds no #16 in the canon and **correctly concludes the migration invented a label** — *the discipline that exists to catch stale counts converts an un-folded instance into an apparent fabrication.*

#### C4 — F6 (note-level): #16's RLS dependency, with Sec's narrowing accepted

My original corollary — that #16's fail-closed depends on RLS invisibility — was **correct but broader than the facts**. Sec's narrowing, adopted: forging `(account_id = A's, users_id = B)` as B **still raises on DATA**, since no account row carries that pair regardless of who is looking, and the tenant conjunct is a second independent layer. Only `(account_id = A's, users_id = A)` degrades to a single layer, because there the pair genuinely matches and **only invisibility** makes `NOT EXISTS` true. Stated in `fn_account_event_matched_account`'s `comment on function`, because **that is where the next author will be standing** — and it extends the symmetric rule with a third clause: *every exemption names its dependency; every fence states what makes it necessary; **and a fence whose sufficiency comes from elsewhere must say so.***

#### C5 — Ledger impacts

- **DEFINER allowlist stays 4 (3 authored)** — the origin fence is INVOKER, like every other fence on this surface. Option A would have moved it to 5; that is the cost B avoids.
- **Decision-3 family: 15 labeled / 12 DDL-realized → 16 / 13**, via C3. **This PR does grow the family**, and the `058` object comments that said otherwise were **object-scoped claims phrased so they could be quoted as PR-scoped ones** — corrected in place, and recorded at Decision 3's fold-in note (e) rather than quietly, since the misrelay is the reusable part.
- **§10 catalogued ledger unchanged at 3** (RT-22 / RT-26 / RT-27; Decision 4 read verbatim). No attribution moves; no surface becomes four-layer. Sec confirmed **3-axis clean** at this review.
- **ADR-011 D1 still does not apply** — unchanged by this amendment. The origin fence narrows *who may write*; it does not make the surface a privileged-context write. **D2 applies**, more strongly than before.

#### C6 — Two consolidations to refuse, recorded because both will read as cleanups

Stated in the DDL as well as here, since a future reader meets the catalog before the ADR:

1. **The direct-insert fence does NOT make `#16` redundant.** They cover **disjoint writers**: `#16` exists for the **migration-role remediation**, which is precisely the writer the direct-insert fence must **exempt**. Two `BEFORE INSERT` triggers on one small table look like duplication; remove either and the other does not cover its case. The rename makes this *more* tempting, not less, because `#16` now fires rarely.
2. **It does NOT make F1's policy conjunct redundant.** A policy survives `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER` and `session_replication_role = replica`; a trigger does not. [Decision 4](#adr-011) commits to fencing at **multiple layers simultaneously**, and *"the new fence covers it"* is exactly the argument that would reopen F1 as a cleanup six months out.

**Related, and it is the sharper half of (2):** because `BEFORE INSERT` triggers fire *before* RLS `WITH CHECK` is evaluated, the direct-insert fence now **masks** F1 for every authenticated POST — so **F1's conjunct is behaviourally unfalsifiable at `authenticated`**. It remains live on the trigger-originated path (the writer is INVOKER, so its INSERT does pass the policy) and it is the layer that survives trigger disablement. **General rule for this table, covering F1 and the `(D1c)` finding together: every `WITH CHECK` conjunct on `pfin.account_event` is behaviourally unfalsifiable at `authenticated`, because BEFORE-trigger fences structurally precede the policy — they must be proven declaratively, via `pg_policies`.**

### Amendment 4 (2026-08-04) — the as-of boundary at `059` is `::date`, and the bare form was a regression

**Status: RATIFIED** — Architect ruling under the standing delegation for predicate granularity; Sec required it be **ruled explicitly at `059` rather than inherited as "free"**, and QA held their assertion pending it rather than pinning current behaviour.

**The question.** `closed_at` is `timestamptz`, `p_as_of` is `date`, so a bare `closed_at > p_as_of` promotes the date to **midnight**: an account closed at 14:00 on `p_as_of` is *included*, one closed at 00:00 is *excluded*.

**I originally recorded this as free-on-value and declined to change it. That was wrong**, and the measurement is what changed it — not the argument, which I had already made in both directions.

| closure time on `p_as_of` | pre-`059` `is_active` | bare `>` | **`::date >`** |
|---|---|---|---|
| 00:00 | EXCLUDED | EXCLUDED | EXCLUDED |
| 14:00 | EXCLUDED | **INCLUDED** | EXCLUDED |
| 23:59 | EXCLUDED | **INCLUDED** | EXCLUDED |

> **The bare form silently changes current-state behaviour AT THIS MIGRATION.** `is_active` went false the instant `closed_at` was set, so an account closed this afternoon left the §2.1.1 headline **immediately**; under the bare form it **stays until midnight**. And it is the **universal case, not an edge** — `fn_close_account` defaults `p_closed_at` to `now()`, so **every app-closed account carries a non-midnight time.** A user closes an account and it is still on the dashboard, which reads as *"the close silently failed"*, on the one control whose entire job is to be believed.

**Why it was so easy to wave through:** NAV **value** is identical under all three forms, because the gate proved zero as of the closure date. **The regression is invisible to every value assertion** and surfaces only in `049`'s **row set** and in account **counts** — which is precisely the class QA's `(A4)` measurement had just exposed, one predicate over.

**Decision — `acc.closed_at::date > p_as_of`, at all three sites** (`049`; `fn_compute_nav`'s securities leg on `acc2`; its cash leg on `acc`). Applied to all three deliberately: **`059` re-points two predicates in `fn_compute_nav` alone**, in different subqueries, so a partial edit is realistic — QA verified each leg reds independently.

Beyond fixing the regression, `::date` puts the read at **the same granularity as everything it reads**: `fn_holdings_as_of(date)`, `fn_account_cash_as_of(date)`, `account_trans.transaction_date`, both checkpoints' `as_of_date`, and the close gate's own `new.closed_at::date` legs. **The time-of-day component of `closed_at` is a precision no other surface acts on** — letting it decide row-set membership would make an otherwise-ignored field load-bearing in exactly one place.

**Rejected — `closed_at::date >= p_as_of`** (an account closed *on* `p_as_of` stays visible for that day). Genuinely defensible: it existed that day, and leg 3 permits activity dated *on* the closure date, so it may have transacted. But it reintroduces the same-day *"close didn't work"* lag for **every** closure including midnight ones, and it disagrees with the retired boolean it replaces.

**Not a one-way door** — a predicate in a function, changeable by a migration. It is a **silent-difference** decision, which is why it gets an explicit ruling and an assertion rather than a default: both forms agree on every value and differ only in row sets, so a wrong choice leaves no footprint. ⚠ **Recorded against the specific rewrite that would undo it:** `closed_at >= (p_as_of + 1)` is behaviourally equivalent and safe, but the tempting **nearby** form — simply dropping the cast for sargability — is the regression. `pfin.account` holds a handful of rows per tenant and carries no index on `closed_at`: nothing to win, a silent behaviour change to lose.

**Verified end-to-end** — `057` → `058` → `059` applied as a chain against a live database in a rolled-back transaction (no reset), then an account closed at 14:00 today: **0 `049` cards today** (matching pre-`059`), **1 card yesterday** (temporally sound — it was open then), one audit row, and `fn_compute_nav(d, true) = fn_compute_nav(d, false)` still holding. *(The chain apply also caught an unescaped apostrophe in a comment string that no amount of reading had — which is the argument for running it rather than reviewing it.)*

---

## ADR-041 — ETL database login identity: a dedicated `pfin_etl` NOINHERIT role, not a shared `authenticator` (SELF-214)

**Date:** 2026-08-02 · **Status:** Accepted — F/CTO ratified 2026-08-02 (option **(B)** dedicated role; option **(A)** share provider-sync's `authenticator` rejected) · **Phase:** 6 Build Loop (V1.1 "Net worth full"; SELF-214; migration `055`)

**Pattern:** Terse-plus — a cluster-level identity decision with a security-posture rationale. **Sec joint-review-mandatory** (a new LOGIN identity + a privileged-role membership grant; raised as B8 at the SELF-214 joint-review).

**Why this is its own ADR and not an ADR-040 amendment.** [ADR-040](#adr-040) is a **nav_daily substrate** decision; this is a **cluster-level identity** with a different lifecycle, a different blast radius, and a different consumer set. `pfin_etl` outlives `nav_daily`, is used by every `workers/etl` path (the in-production BLS + FMP + CPI-U ingest as well as the W-1 NAV cron), and would have to survive any future drop-replace of `nav_daily`. It is also not an [ADR-023](#adr-023) amendment: ADR-023 pinned the **write** role-of-record (`service_role`) and the Data-API transport; it never decided **which login identity** reaches the database, which is exactly the gap Sec's B1 exposed. This ADR closes that gap and *amends ADR-023's C1 coupling* as a consequence, but the decision itself is new territory.

**Decision — option (B).** Create **`pfin_etl`**, a dedicated **`NOINHERIT`** login role (migration `055`), as the `workers/etl` container's database identity. It is granted membership in **`service_role`** (privileged writes, per the ADR-023 write role-of-record) and **`authenticated`** (the W-1 session-impersonation read path), and **nothing else**: not superuser, not owner, not `BYPASSRLS`, no direct table or schema privilege in `pfin`. Its entire reach is via an explicit `SET ROLE`. **The migration creates it `NOLOGIN` with NO PASSWORD** — the role ships inert; an operator switches it on at deploy time with a single atomic `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD '<Coolify secret>'`. So `rolcanlogin` is **FALSE at migration time** and TRUE only in a provisioned environment.

**Sub-decision — `NOLOGIN`-then-flip, not `LOGIN`-with-no-password (Sec NOTE on `055`, adopted 2026-08-02; non-blocking, adopted on the merits).** The first draft created the role `LOGIN` with no password and argued the pre-provision state was fail-closed because *"a LOGIN role with no password cannot authenticate under scram-sha-256."* **That claim is true in production but does not generalize, and the gap was measured rather than theorized:** the local stack's `pg_hba.conf` grants `trust` on `127.0.0.1/32`, `::1/128` and `local`, and **`trust` never consults a password at all** — so a passwordless `LOGIN` role is reachable there with **no credential**. The old shape outsourced its fail-closed property to a file outside this repo that differs across production, local dev, and CI. `NOLOGIN` removes the dependency: `rolcanlogin` is checked from the **role attribute itself, before any authentication method**, so the role is unreachable under `trust`, `scram-sha-256`, or anything else — **true by construction**, which is how every other fence in this project is built. It costs nothing operationally (`LOGIN` and `PASSWORD` are set in the *same* statement, so they are never separated in time), and it improves the botched-deploy failure mode: a skipped deploy step leaves the role rejected by its own attribute rather than `LOGIN`-but-hopefully-unauthenticatable. **Rejected alternative:** keep `LOGIN` plus a deploy-time operator assertion that the password was set before container start — weaker, because it depends on a human check rather than on construction.

**Sub-decision — role-naming convention for worker login identities (Architect call, recorded so it cannot drift).** The cluster is about to carry two near-identical NOINHERIT worker roles from two separately-reasoned decisions: `pfin_etl` (created here) and **`pfin_worker`**, forward-flagged at **[ADR-019](#adr-019)'s Condition C2** as V1.x hardening for `workers/provider-sync`. *(Note the provenance precisely: C2 lives in **ADR-019**, the monorepo/login-role note — not ADR-023, which pinned the write role and never named a login identity.)* Two roles is correct — that is exactly the principal separation B8 just ratified — but `pfin_worker` is a **bad name**, because *every* one of these is a worker: `workers/etl` is a worker, `workers/provider-sync` is a worker, the PDF worker is a worker. A generic name on the second member of a growing family is how naming drifts. **Convention, effective now: `pfin_<workers/ subdirectory>`.** So `workers/etl` → **`pfin_etl`** (already correct, no change) and `workers/provider-sync` → **`pfin_provider_sync`**, which **supersedes the `pfin_worker` name** in ADR-019 C2 when that role is adopted. The convention is mechanical — the role name is derivable from the directory with no judgement call — so it cannot drift and needs no future adjudication. ADR-019 C2's *substance* (a dedicated NOINHERIT role for provider-sync, Sec-joint-review-mandatory on adoption) is **unchanged**; only the name is pinned, and the rename rides C2's existing Sec gate rather than needing its own. **`pfin_etl` and `pfin_provider_sync` are deliberately separate roles, not converged into one shared worker role** — collapsing them would reintroduce exactly the shared-credential blast radius and loss-of-independent-revocation that (A) was rejected for.

**Sub-decision — ADR-019 C2 is PROMOTED from "V1.x hardening" to a Phase-7 deploy gate (Architect call on Sec's recommendation; NOT SELF-214 scope).** Sec's read, which I adopt: **B8's argument applies MORE strongly to `workers/provider-sync` than to the ETL it was won for.** provider-sync holds `PLAID_SECRET`, decrypts SD-03 access tokens, and writes the tenant-scoped `account` / `account_trans` surface — on blast-radius grounds it is the higher-value target of the two, and it is the one still sharing `authenticator` with PostgREST. Leaving it on the shared credential *after* ratifying (B) for the lower-value container is not a defensible end-state. **But it is not SELF-214 work:** it is a different container, a different PR, and a credential that does not exist yet. Promoting it to a **Phase-7 deploy gate** puts it exactly where the provisioning happens anyway — `pfin_provider_sync` gets created and its password set in the same deploy pass that switches on `pfin_etl`, so the cluster reaches a consistent role-graph in one operation rather than carrying a half-applied convention across releases. ADR-019 C2's Sec-joint-review-mandatory condition is **unchanged** and still gates adoption. **Tracked at [`BACKLOG.md`](BACKLOG.md) §7.6 item S5** (repo-versioned per [ADR-017](#adr-017) Decision 2 — the Linear workspace is at its free-issue limit; promotes at the first milestone-rotation after the limit lifts). **Until it lands, ADR-023's C1 rotation coupling continues to bind PostgREST + provider-sync** (the narrowing recorded above removes only the ETL); C1 is fully discharged only when `pfin_provider_sync` replaces `authenticator` in provider-sync's `PFIN_DB_USER`.

**Alternatives considered.**
- **A — reuse provider-sync's `authenticator`.** Rejected on three grounds. **(1) Blast radius:** `authenticator` is the credential *PostgREST itself authenticates with*, so compromising a long-running batch ETL container would yield the identity fronting the **entire public Data API** (ADR-023 exposed `pfin` to PostgREST). A batch container and an API gateway have materially different exposure profiles; one credential collapses them into a single failure. **(2) No independent revocation:** the ETL could not be revoked or rotated without simultaneously downing PostgREST *and* provider-sync — an identity you cannot revoke in isolation is not really a separate principal. **(3) It contradicts a committed contract:** `secrets-manifest.yml` already states verbatim that `PFIN_DB_PASSWORD` carries *"DIFFERENT credential VALUES per container: ETL uses its own login credential"*. (A) would have required editing that commitment to match a shortcut; (B) realizes it as written. *(Sec and team-lead reached (B) independently before comparing reasoning.)*
- **B — dedicated `pfin_etl` NOINHERIT login role (CHOSEN).** Independently revocable (`ALTER ROLE pfin_etl NOLOGIN` stops the ETL and nothing else), independently rotatable, and honours the manifest. Cost: one more credential to provision and rotate — accepted as the correct price for principal separation.
- **C — let the ETL keep an owner-class login (e.g. `postgres`).** Rejected, and it is the outcome the whole SELF-214 B1 thread exists to prevent: under an owner-class identity the `054` `grant insert … to service_role` goes **inert** (ownership authorizes the write) and the append-only triggers become **bypassable by the writer** via `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER` or `session_replication_role = 'replica'` — both reproduced live in a rolled-back transaction at the joint-review.

**`rolinherit = false` alone is NOT sufficient on PG 16+ (found by QA; verified independently 2026-08-02 on PG 17.6).** Since PG 16 inheritance is **per-membership**: `GRANT service_role TO pfin_etl WITH INHERIT TRUE` confers privileges implicitly — defeating this ADR's entire posture — **while `rolinherit` still reads `false`**. Measured: default grant → `rolinherit=false / USAGE=false / MEMBER=true` (correct); `WITH INHERIT TRUE` → `rolinherit=false / USAGE=true / MEMBER=true` (defeated). **Both checks are therefore required, and they govern different things:** `rolinherit = false` governs *future* memberships (the default applied when a grant omits `INHERIT`), while `pg_has_role(…,'MEMBER') = true AND pg_has_role(…,'USAGE') = false` governs the *existing* ones — that pair is what actually proves no privilege is held without a `SET ROLE`. `055`'s grants deliberately omit any `INHERIT` clause so the role default applies, and SECURITY §4.5 RT-31 leg (c) plus QA's battery assert both. The MEMBER/USAGE pair is load-bearing, not stylistic; trimming it as redundant re-opens the hole.

**Why NOINHERIT is load-bearing (not stylistic).** With `rolinherit = f` the role holds the *privileges* of neither membership until it issues an explicit `SET ROLE`. Two properties follow: **(i) fail-closed, loudly** — a code path that forgets `set local role service_role` gets a hard `42501` at the ACL rather than silently succeeding while over-privileged (this is precisely Sec's B1(c) functional finding about the W-1 append, caught on paper rather than in production); **(ii) the elevated window is bounded by the `SET ROLE`, not by the connection lifetime.** It also mirrors the `authenticator` posture Backend's worker code is **already written against**, so option (B) requires **no Backend code change** — the two memberships granted are the same two (A) would have relied on.

**Why this makes `054`'s fences real.** `pfin_etl` is neither table owner nor superuser, so it can neither `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER` (must be owner) nor set `session_replication_role` (must be superuser) — the only two ways to suppress a trigger. Consequently `054`'s append-only immutability fences **and** its B7 write-tenant binding fence are **un-bypassable by the writer** under this identity. That is the security argument for the migration.

**Consequences.**
- **[ADR-023](#adr-023) condition C1 — rotation coupling narrows from three consumers to two.** C1 (provider-sync's `PFIN_DB_PASSWORD` == the `authenticator` password == PostgREST's credential, so rotating either forces a **coordinated** redeploy of both) now binds **PostgREST + provider-sync only**. The ETL is **out** of that coupling: `pfin_etl`'s password rotates independently, restarting only the ETL container. C1 is **narrowed in scope, not weakened or rescinded** for the consumers it still covers.
- **ADR-011 Decision 1 clause (b)** ("writes execute under `service_role`") stays satisfied **literally** — `pfin_etl` reaches `service_role` by `SET LOCAL ROLE`, exactly as ADR-023 specifies. Migration `008`'s least-privilege per-table `service_role` grant architecture remains the **single** place table privileges are decided; `pfin_etl` is deliberately granted **no** direct table or schema privilege, so that decision surface is not forked.
- **Deploy-time handoff (DevOps + Backend, Phase 7 / first ETL deploy) — TWO statements, and the ORDER is load-bearing (Sec joint-review B10, 2026-08-02):** `\password pfin_etl` (psql meta-command: prompts, computes the SCRAM verifier **client-side**, sets *only* the password while the role is still `NOLOGIN` and therefore inert), **then** `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl LOGIN` (carries no secret). The operator must be superuser or hold `CREATEROLE`/`ADMIN OPTION`, since `\password` is `ALTER USER` underneath. **The single atomic `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD '<plaintext>'` that this ADR originally mandated is now PROHIBITED** — measured, not theorized: `log_statement = ddl` and Postgres does **not** redact passwords from the statement log, so that form writes the ETL credential into the server log **in cleartext**. An interim mitigation ("use an interactive session, never `psql -c`") was also insufficient: it dodges *shell* history but lands in psql's own plaintext `~/.psql_history`. **Be precise about what `\password` buys — do NOT write "the secret isn't logged":** it still sends `ALTER USER … PASSWORD 'SCRAM-SHA-256$4096:…'`, which is DDL and **is** logged. What changes is *what* is logged. Plaintext never leaves the client (so it reaches neither the wire, the log, nor `.psql_history`); the logged **verifier is not a usable credential** (it holds `StoredKey` + `ServerKey`, while a client proof needs `ClientKey`, and `StoredKey = H(ClientKey)` is not invertible); the residual is an **offline attack** bounded by secret entropy and the measured 4096 iterations — acceptable for a high-entropy generated secret and categorically better than cleartext, which is replayable immediately. **Splitting the statement does not reopen the `NOLOGIN` window**, because of the ordering: the credential lands while the role is still `NOLOGIN`, then `LOGIN` flips onto an already-credentialed role, so *LOGIN-with-no-password never exists at any instant*. The two-step is safe **because of** the order, not despite the split — which also means **running step 2 without step 1 is the one dangerous ordering**, and it is exactly what `055`'s re-apply WARNING branch detects. Rotation = `\password pfin_etl` + restart the ETL container only. Revocation = `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl NOLOGIN`. Then set `PFIN_DB_USER=pfin_etl` (non-secret username) + `PFIN_DB_PASSWORD` (production_only secret). Rotation = `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH PASSWORD '<new>'` + restart the ETL container only. Revocation = `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl NOLOGIN`. Skipping the deploy step yields a loud `role "pfin_etl" is not permitted to log in` at connect — an outage, never an exposure. **`055` deliberately does NOT re-apply attributes to a pre-existing role** (after deploy the role is legitimately `LOGIN`, and "correcting" it on re-run would take the live ETL down); instead it reports the found attributes and raises a WARNING on the two states that would defeat the posture — `INHERIT`, and `LOGIN` with no password. Password state is read from `pg_authid`, since `pg_roles.rolpassword` is a literal `'********'` and is useless for the check.
- **All ledgers FLAT** (each confirmed against the canonical ADR-011 bodies read verbatim 2026-08-02, not cited from memory): §10 catalogued instances **3** (RT-22 first / RT-26 second / RT-27 third — a new LOGIN identity is a credential-posture change, **not** a catalogued fence instance; `pfin_etl` is a DB-layer cluster role, not the RT-26 code-layer key allowlist, not RT-22, not RT-27; no layer attribution moves); SECURITY DEFINER allowlist **4** (`055` authors **no function at all**); Decision-3 family **15 labeled / 12 DDL-realized** (`055` creates no table, column, or FK-shaped reference); RT-26 `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` allowlist **unchanged** (the ETL holds a direct-Postgres credential and stays off that code-layer allowlist, same posture as provider-sync).
- **SECURITY doc (Sec-owned; routed, not edited here):** SD-24 and RT-31 currently frame the ETL login role as an **open deployment decision**. This ADR **closes** it. Both entries need a wording update, and RT-31's instruction to assert against *both* an owner-class and a `service_role` write shape can narrow now that the identity is decided.

**Approved by:** Founder/CTO (2026-08-02).

**Cross-references.** [ADR-023](#adr-023) (write role-of-record + the C1 rotation coupling this narrows; `008` least-privilege grants) · [ADR-040](#adr-040) (`nav_daily` + W-1; the credential-model sub-decision this realizes, and the B7 fence this makes un-bypassable) · [ADR-011](#adr-011) Decision 1 (privileged-context-write, clause (b)) / Decision 3 (unchanged) / Decision 4 (§10 — stays 3) / Decision 9 (DEFINER allowlist — stays 4) / **Lock 11** (INVOKER read-composition — the `authenticated` membership serves it) / **Lock 13 mod #3** (`TenantBoundConnection`) · [ADR-019](#adr-019) (`workers/etl/`) · [ADR-021](#adr-021) (greenfield — the credential does not exist until deploy) · `secrets-manifest.yml` (`PFIN_DB_PASSWORD` per-container values; `PFIN_DB_USER` non-secret) · migrations `055` (this role) / `054` (the fences it protects) / `008` (where table grants stay decided) · SECURITY §4.4 SD-24 / §4.5 RT-31.

---

## ADR-040 — `pfin.nav_daily` net-worth-trend substrate: precomputed append-only checkpoint (Option B) + W-1 impersonation worker model (SELF-214)

**Date:** 2026-08-02 · **Status:** Accepted — F/CTO ratified 2026-08-02 (Option B; A derived-view + C matview/DEFINER rejected · worker model W-1; W-2 new-DEFINER + W-3 python-recompute rejected) — **amended same-day 2026-08-02** with the Sec-joint-review dispositions (credential model B1; write-tenant binding fence B7 (c′); D1(d) deferral record B3; RT-28→RT-31; SD-24 tier `medium`→`high`) · **Phase:** 6 Build Loop (V1.1 "Net worth full"; SELF-214; migration `054`)

**Pattern:** Terse-plus — a base-table substrate decision with a one-way-door storage-shape choice + a precedent-setting worker sub-decision. **Sec joint-review-mandatory** (tenant-scoped financial data + new RLS + immutability triggers + worker tenant-binding).

**Decision:** Persist the §2.1.2 net-worth trajectory as **`pfin.nav_daily`** — a precomputed **append-only** table, one row per `(users_id, nav_date)` = the NAV **frozen at that day's close** by the cron worker calling the locked INVOKER `fn_compute_nav(current_date, true)` (`050` active-only). `users_id uuid` (sole tenant anchor, direct-owner RLS `= auth.uid()`); `nav_value` finiteness-fenced (NaN + ±Infinity); `UNIQUE(users_id, nav_date)`; Lock 10 mod #8 append-only cross-tier triggers (UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE blocked for authenticated **and** service_role). Migration `054`. **Forward-only** — the trajectory accumulates from first run; no historical backfill in V1.1.

**Why precomputed (not derived-on-read):** `050`'s **temporal constraint** — `fn_compute_nav`'s active-only scope is sound **only at `p_as_of = current_date`** (`is_active` is current-state; filtering it into a past as-of retroactively rewrites history). `050` explicitly forward-named this table: history must come from **frozen checkpoints**, not on-the-fly `fn_compute_nav(<past>, true)`. A precomputed row captures the true as-of-that-day active set, immutably.

**Alternatives considered (AC1):**
- **A — derived view / recompute-on-read.** Rejected: temporally unsound (can't reconstruct a past day's active set from current `is_active` — the `050` constraint), and recomputing a full NAV per trajectory point per page-load is expensive.
- **B — precomputed append-only table (CHOSEN).** Frozen-at-compute-time (temporally correct by construction), cheap reads (indexed `(users_id, nav_date)`), audit-class immutability. Cost: a daily write path (the cron worker) + a new SD/RT SECURITY entry.
- **C — materialized view + SECURITY DEFINER refresh.** Rejected: a DEFINER refresh over a financial calc (against Lock 11 INVOKER read-composition) + refresh-coupling/staleness; the append-only table gives frozen history a matview can't (a matview recomputes, losing the as-of-that-day active set).

**Sub-decision — worker model W-1 (precedent-setting; the first live per-user ETL write path):** the cron worker binds each tenant via `TenantBoundConnection.for_tenant()` and computes NAV by **session-impersonation** (`set local role authenticated` + a synthetic `request.jwt.claims.sub = <users_id>`), reusing the **locked INVOKER `fn_compute_nav`** under RLS, tears the impersonation down, then appends the checkpoint **as `service_role`, reached via `SET LOCAL ROLE service_role` from a login session held as `authenticator`** — *not* by connecting as `service_role`, which is impossible (see the credential-model sub-decision below). **Because W-1 is precedent** — this ADR calls it the durable per-user worker pattern, and the next per-user worker will copy this text rather than re-derive it — the role naming here is load-bearing documentation, not a code comment: a future session reading "INSERTs as service_role" would design the next worker around a login identity that cannot exist.
- **W-2 — new `SECURITY DEFINER fn_compute_nav_for_user(uuid, date)`.** Rejected: **DEFINER allowlist 4→5** (Sec veto surface) + **duplicates** `050`'s valuation → INVOKER/DEFINER drift on a financial calc (exactly what Lock 11 steers away from).
- **W-3 — recompute NAV in Python.** Rejected: a third valuation implementation (worst drift); abandons the DB as single valuation source of truth.
- **Consequence:** W-1 keeps the **DEFINER allowlist at 4** (authors no new function) and reuses the Sec-reviewed valuation. It **firms the dormant `TenantBoundConnection` per-tenant assertion contract** (nav_daily is its first live caller) and establishes session-impersonation as the durable per-user worker pattern. **Load-bearing worker edge (for the worker's Sec joint-review):** the impersonation session must claim **`aal2`** or a totp/passkey user's underlying reads (025-aal2-gated) filter to zero → a frozen NAV of 0.

**Sub-decision — the ETL write-path credential model (F/CTO-ratified 2026-08-02 at the Sec joint-review; B1).** The ETL **logs in under a NOINHERIT broker role** (`rolinherit = f`, `rolcanlogin = t`, not `BYPASSRLS`, not the `pfin` owner, member of `authenticated` so W-1's impersonation works under it) and **executes privileged writes as `service_role` via `SET LOCAL ROLE service_role`**, adopting the [ADR-023](#adr-023) write-role-of-record precedent already deployed at `workers/provider-sync`. **Which** NOINHERIT role was decided immediately after, at the same joint-review: **not** provider-sync's shared `authenticator` but a **dedicated `pfin_etl`** — see **[ADR-041](#adr-041)** (option (B), F/CTO-ratified 2026-08-02, migration `055`), which supersedes any reading of this sub-decision that names `authenticator` as the ETL's login identity. Everything below holds identically under either, because the property that matters is *NOINHERIT non-owner broker + writes as `service_role`*, not the role's name. **What this corrects:** `service_role` has `rolcanlogin = f` and can therefore **never** be a direct-Postgres worker's login identity, so the "the worker connects as `service_role`" framing this ADR and `054` previously carried named a **transport-impossible role**. **What it buys** (measured live, read-only / rolled-back): `054`'s `grant insert … to service_role` becomes **load-bearing exactly as its header claims** rather than decorative; `service_role` is neither table owner nor superuser, so it can neither `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER` nor set `session_replication_role` — making the append-only fences **un-bypassable by the writer**; and an owner-class login (e.g. `postgres`) would **invert every one of those properties** (grant inert, both trigger-bypass mechanisms succeed — reproduced live). **Consequence for ADR-011 Decision 1 clause (b)** ("writes execute under `service_role`"): satisfied **literally**, not merely in substance. **Consequence for the worker code:** under a NOINHERIT login, `impersonate()`'s `reset role` leaves the session holding no privileges, so the append **must** issue `set local role service_role` before the INSERT or it fails `42501` — a functional requirement on Backend's half, not a doc change.

**Sub-decision — write-tenant binding fence, remedy (c′) (F/CTO-ratified 2026-08-02; Sec joint-review B7). One-way-door-adjacent, which is why it is fenced rather than accepted.** `054` carries a SELECT policy only — **no INSERT policy, no `WITH CHECK`** — and the writer bypasses RLS, so write-path tenant correctness was an **application-layer guarantee only** (Decision 1 clause (c), realized in `TenantBoundConnection`'s per-tenant assertion) with nothing behind it. That assertion structurally cannot catch this class: it compares the statement against the same variable that would be wrong. **Fence (c′):** inside the impersonated block the worker executes `select set_config('app.nav_computed_for', auth.uid()::text, true)`, and a `BEFORE INSERT` `SECURITY INVOKER` trigger (`pfin.fn_nav_daily_assert_computed_for`, migration `054`) asserts `new.users_id::text` equals that transaction-local GUC, failing closed on unset / empty / NULL / mismatch. **The security-critical property is the capture source:** the GUC is taken from `auth.uid()` **as the database resolved it**, never from the app's own variable — that is what proves the row's tenant *is* the tenant served, and it is what closes the `request.jwt.claim.sub` singular-GUC path (`auth.uid()` prefers the legacy singular GUC over the plural claims blob; a session- or role-scoped singular GUC would otherwise serve **one** tenant's NAV for **every** tenant, with no code bug and no app-layer assertion failure). **Alternatives:** *(b′) transaction-declared tenant* — worker sets the GUC from its own variable before impersonating; rejected, same cost for strictly less guarantee (it closes the refactor/loop-variable class but **not** the singular-GUC path, since both sides derive from the app). *(d) accept as application-layer-only* — rejected; legitimate and non-vetoed, but rows here are append-only and forward-only, so a checkpoint written under the wrong `users_id` is **removable only by a migration**. **Consequences:** DEFINER allowlist **stays 4** (the fence is INVOKER); it is **not** a Decision-3 instance (it validates the row's own anchor against the served tenant, not a referenced row's tenant scope) so the family stays **15 labeled / 12 DDL-realized**; it is **not** a §10 catalogued instance (ledger stays **3**); and **every** INSERT path — worker, QA fixture, seed, any future backfill — must now set the GUC in the same transaction, which is deliberate (an unfenced convenience path would re-open the gap).

**Consequences.**
- **ADR-011 Decision 1 clause (d) — EXPLICITLY DEFERRED, not satisfied, not waived (Sec joint-review B3; recorded per Sec's requirement).** Decision 1 commits non-JWT writes to a four-clause discipline and states that a new privileged-context surface **MUST** adopt it at design time; it further names **"Lock 7 NAV worker"** under *Confirmed reusability*, so this surface is one of the four D1 was generalized from, not merely covered by it. Clause-by-clause at ship: **(a)** ingress under no JWT — satisfied. **(b)** writes execute under `service_role` — satisfied **literally** under the credential-model sub-decision above (Sec's interim "violated in the letter" ruling was withdrawn at joint-review v3). **(c)** tenant correctness from code, not RLS — satisfied, and now additionally proven at the DB layer by the (c′) fence. **(d) explicit audit log capturing the tenant-resolution chain — NOT SATISFIED.** `emit_audit_log` raises `NotImplementedError`; the worker emits no audit row. **Blocking dependency:** the `plaid_sync_audit`-class audit table **plus** the general same-transaction audit-log insert helper — which is the **reserved, already-allowlisted 4th DEFINER slot**, still unauthored (scoped as SELF-201 Task #7 per [ADR-026](#adr-026) — **but note SELF-201 itself closed Done on 2026-07-06 with that task deferred out of it, verified against live Linear 2026-08-03, so the helper has no live tracker; [`BACKLOG.md`](BACKLOG.md) §7.6 **S7** is the standing one**); authoring it moves **no** ledger, the slot is already held at 4. **What is and is not mitigated:** `nav_daily` is itself immutable and records the tenant-resolution *outcome* (`users_id`, `nav_date`, `nav_value`, `created_at`); what is absent is the *chain* — run identity, which tenants were enumerated, per-tenant success/failure, and the fact of a checkpoint having been **attempted and failed** (a tenant that errors leaves no durable trace at all). **Risk is forensic, not exploitative**, which is why this is a recorded deferral rather than a ship-block. **Tracked at [`BACKLOG.md`](BACKLOG.md) §7.6 item S7** — *not* in Linear. **Back-annotation 2026-08-02:** this bullet originally read *"a Linear follow-up issue is required (label `joint-review:sec`)"*, which was **un-dischargeable** — the Linear workspace is at its free-issue limit and creation fails outright for everyone, so the ADR asserted an obligation that could not be met and pointed at nothing. Per [ADR-017](#adr-017) Decision 2, `BACKLOG.md` §7 is the repo-versioned staging queue for V1 work not currently in Linear, which is exactly this condition; S7 carries the `joint-review:sec` framing and promotes to Linear at the first milestone-rotation after the limit lifts. *(Correction class: a **forward-looking claim that became false** — distinct from a point-in-time statement, which stands as written. Same class Sec required corrected on the column-REVOKE rationale.)*
- Migration `054` — **three** SECURITY INVOKER trigger functions (the two immutability fences + the (c′) write-tenant binding fence), so the DEFINER allowlist is **unchanged at 4**; `users_id` = sole own tenant anchor → **Decision-3 family unchanged at 15 labeled / 12 DDL-realized** (not a cross-tenant FK-bypass instance, `024`/`009` shape; the (c′) fence is Decision-1 clause (c), not Decision 3); §10 catalogued ledger **unchanged at 3** (RT-22 first / RT-26 second / RT-27 third; 3-axis clean — the `service_role` INSERT grant is a DB-ACL, not RT-26, and no catalogued instance's layer attribution moves). **aal2 step-up backstop INHERITED** on the SELECT policy (C3 / `025` — nav_daily is sensitive tenant-owned, not an exclusion). **`054` was amended IN PLACE** on the unmerged branch to add the (c′) fence and the corrected credential-model language — it had never been merged or applied to any environment, so there was no applied state to drop-replace; the file's header records that reasoning so it is not misread as an edited-live-migration violation.
- **SECURITY doc (Sec-canonical; LANDED by Sec at the 2026-08-02 joint-review):** SD matrix **+1** = **SD-24** — nav_daily; **`high` severity** (Sec bumped this ADR's proposed `medium` per the **SD-13** derivative-persistence rule: `fn_compute_nav`'s sources include SD-00, and a per-day net-worth *series* is more disclosive than any single ledger row), storage class **`tenant-scoped-derivative`** (an existing closed-enum member on the SD-12 precedent — **no ADR-008 amendment**), retention `indefinite` per §4.6. RT catalog **+1** = **RT-31** (`high`, deliberately **not** `critical` — §4.5 locks the expected V1 critical count at 2, RT-02 + RT-05). **SLOT CORRECTION:** this ADR originally proposed **RT-28**, which was **already allocated** to the Plaid Link CSP allowlist ([ADR-028](#adr-028) / SELF-212); the catalog stood at RT-30, so the correct slot is **RT-31**. Had the proposal landed, §4.5 would carry two RT-28s.
- **Backend (parallel, not this migration):** the W-1 daily-NAV worker in `workers/etl/` — Architect + Sec joint-review its tenant-binding (impersonation + aal2-claim + audit-log) after. Backend additionally owns the **worker half of the (c′) contract** (the `set_config('app.nav_computed_for', auth.uid()::text, true)` inside the impersonated block — **GUC name pinned**), the `set local role service_role` before the append, and the corrected credential-model language in the `workers/etl` docstrings + `.env.example`.
- **QA** two-tenant battery ships same-PR: cross-tenant SELECT fails closed (owner-only + aal2); authenticated INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE denied **at the ACL** (no write grant — Postgres checks the ACL before RLS, so the immutability trigger is never reached on that path; proving the trigger load-bearing needs a TEST-ONLY rolled-back grant); UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE blocked at the privileged tier that actually reaches the table (append-only; the row-level and statement-level fences raise **distinct** messages — assert both, and run the TRUNCATE leg at the owner tier); finiteness CHECK rejects NaN + ±Infinity; **and the (c′) fence: INSERT rejected on unset / empty / mismatched `app.nav_computed_for`, accepted when correctly bound** — note every fixture INSERT must now set that GUC. Verify **non-destructively** (apply-in-txn + rollback); a full `db reset` destroys the F/CTO's active local test data.
- **Forward-flag:** historical backfill (a seeded pre-launch trajectory) is deferred — if ever wanted, the temporally-sound path is `fn_compute_nav(d, false)` (all-accounts as-of), a distinct future decision.

- **Write-path idempotency = the TARGETED `on conflict (users_id, nav_date) do nothing`, admitted by a COLUMN-LEVEL grant (Sec joint-review B9 ruling, 2026-08-02).** `054` originally granted `service_role` INSERT alone while its own CONTRACT specified a *targeted* `ON CONFLICT` — but a targeted conflict specification requires `SELECT` on its arbiter columns, so the documented idempotency mechanism raised `42501` on **every** production run. Ruling: keep the targeted statement and add **`grant select (users_id, nav_date) on pfin.nav_daily to service_role`** — exactly those two columns, paired with a belt-and-braces `revoke all (nav_value)`. **Rejected: a table-level `grant select`** — precisely *because* `service_role` bypasses RLS the ACL is the only remaining fence on it, so "RLS doesn't constrain it, so the ACL needn't either" would justify granting SELECT on every table in `pfin` and dissolves `008`'s per-table least-privilege model; it would also hand over every tenant's full trajectory, the exact datum SD-24 is rated HIGH to protect. **Rejected: an untargeted `on conflict do nothing`** (which needs no grant) — it silently absorbs *any* unique/exclusion violation, so a future constraint would be swallowed on a financial-correctness path; that asks `054` to violate its own stated fail-loud principle (`fn_nav_daily_block_mutation`: *"raise, NOT return null — return null would silently no-op the row and read as 'succeeded'"*). **Not a novel relaxation** — column-level grants are the established least-privilege idiom in this schema: `026:196` (`grant select (users_id), update (mfa_policy) … to service_role`), plus the multi-column `007:214` / `015:225` grants that withhold a Vault secret handle and pair with a belt-and-braces REVOKE. **Residual, stated because `026` is NOT an exact match:** `026` grants one column and no sequence; this grants two, the second temporal — so `service_role` gains, cross-tenant, checkpoint *existence*, first-valued date, and every *gap* in the series (i.e. when a tenant onboarded and when the cron failed them). It gains **no monetary value**. The zero-marginal-disclosure argument covers the tenant roster (`service_role` already reads `pfin.account`) but **not** the date sequence. **Widening the grant — any extra column, or a move to table-level — is Sec joint-review-mandatory.** **No `revoke` statement accompanies the grant (Sec, B9 tail):** an interim draft carried `revoke all (nav_value)` as belt-and-braces; Sec ruled it REMOVED because *an executable statement that enforces nothing is worse than a comment saying the same thing — code implies enforcement*, so a reader seeing a `revoke` infers a protection that does not exist. It was also powerless in both directions (Postgres has no negative grants — measured: a later table-level `GRANT SELECT` confers on every column regardless of an earlier column revoke, and a column revoke cannot claw back from a table grant). The intent survives as a comment; the actual controls are the narrowness of the column grant, the Sec-joint-review widening trigger, and QA's two negative assertions. **Pre-existing drift flagged beyond this PR:** `007:217`, `015:229` and `008:104-105` each justify a paired column REVOKE as ensuring the withheld column "is not reachable via a broad grant" — the same false claim, on two Vault-credential surfaces, with `008`'s instance written as the discharge of ADR-023 Sec condition **C3**. Nothing is exposed (the column-scoped GRANT is the real fence in all three), but the comments promise a forward protection that does not exist. **And C3's own text settles the disposition:** ADR-023 records C3 verbatim as *"`plaid_items.access_token_secret_id` stays withheld from the `authenticated` grant (**007 column-scoped SELECT**; **008 re-asserts the REVOKE**)"* — so the governing condition **already names the column-scoped GRANT as the mechanism** and `008` as merely a re-assertion. "C3's substance is intact" is therefore not a mitigation argued after the fact; it is what C3 said from the start, and the `008` comment drifted away from its own governing condition. Correction tracked at [`BACKLOG.md`](BACKLOG.md) §7.6 item **S2** — **disposition (c) decided**: fold the C3 prose correction into [ADR-023](#adr-023) itself, which is right precisely because the defect is prose overstepping its governing condition, not a mechanism to repair. Item **S3** carries the companion fix to `007` / `015`, and PM's framing there is better than mine: **INVERT those comments from reassurance to warning** rather than merely correcting them — a comment that says "this does NOT protect you" is worth more than one that says nothing, because it pre-empts the next engineer re-adding the false fence. Separately, Sec ruled that correcting prose does **not** discharge C3 going forward and required a pgTAP assertion that `authenticated` holds no table-level SELECT on `plaid_items` / `linked_source` — the fence the comment falsely promised (QA-owned; §7.6 item **S1**).
- **New cross-artifact invariant, and why it is fenced rather than merely documented.** The targeted form couples two artifacts in two files that must move together: *the grant's column list must match the arbiter columns of the UNIQUE constraint the worker names in its `ON CONFLICT` target.* The fence is Sec's requirement that QA's battery exercise the **production statement verbatim** — change the constraint without the grant and the battery fails `42501`; change the arbiter without a matching unique index and it fails `42P10`. Either way the PR that breaks the pairing is the PR that goes red, so the invariant cannot drift silently. The corollary is a standing instruction: **do not "simplify" the battery to a plain INSERT** — that is exactly what let the original defect ship green through four reviewers. Relatedly, the **one-arbiter invariant** (that `UNIQUE (users_id, nav_date)` is the sole conflict-arbitrable constraint) is retained in `054` as **documentation only** — it was load-bearing under the rejected untargeted form; under the targeted form a second constraint raises `23505` loudly instead of being absorbed. And `DO UPDATE` is unavailable here as a **correctness bar, not a style preference**: it takes an UPDATE path, which the append-only trigger blocks by design, because a checkpoint is a historical fact and is never revised.
- **Citation discipline, and the ordinal-label hazard (adopted at SELF-214).** Two rules, both earned here rather than assumed. **(i) Verify a citation before relying on it.** Three times in this one review, opening a cited source changed what it supported: `026:196` held as cited (fine); `007:217` / `015:229` / `008:104-105` turned out to carry the *same false claim* the citation was offered to validate; and ADR-023's C3 text turned out to *already* name the column-scoped GRANT as the mechanism, which strengthened the finding rather than weakening it. The discipline pays out precisely when it costs you the point — and it made a **pre-existing error checkable that nobody was looking for**, which is a stronger argument for it than the usual drift-prevention one. **(ii) Ordinal labels in a shared list are a SHARED MUTABLE NAMESPACE.** Every reference to `§7.6 S<n>` is a claim about *position*; any insertion into that list silently invalidates every existing citation — no error, no broken link, just a pointer that now resolves to something else. This bit immediately: `BACKLOG.md` §7.6 grew 4 → 8 entries and **all four** of this ADR-pair's citations became wrong, one of them resolving to a real entry *about a different subject* — which reads as correct to anyone who does not open it. That is the same defect class as a comment describing a fence that does not exist, but cross-file and with nothing to trip over. **Positional labels degrade in BOTH directions:** the same renumber produced a *wrong* citation (a stale `S3` resolving to a real entry about a different subject) **and** a *false alarm* (an observer working from a remembered count flagged a mismatch that did not exist, because the list had grown again since their last read). They mislead the reader who trusts them and they waste the reader who checks. **Compounding failure mode — fix the primary, miss the secondary.** The stale citation above was corrected in its first occurrence while a *second* reference to the same entry, four sentences later inside the same bullet, was left stale — and the correct replacement token (`S7`) sat beside a *legitimately different* `S3` using the identical verb, so a naive find-and-replace would have broken the good one. Prefer stable identifiers over positional ones for anything cited from outside the list; where positional labels are unavoidable, **re-verify every citation programmatically at each edit cycle** — resolve each label against the live list's headings rather than eyeballing the one that was flagged.
- **Standing verification discipline for privileged-worker surfaces — EXECUTE THE ASSEMBLED STATEMENT SEQUENCE, not its parts (adopted at SELF-214; this is the durable lesson and it generalizes with W-1 itself).** The `ON CONFLICT` privilege defect (B9) passed **four** independent reviewers because every artifact was individually defensible: the Architect wrote `grant insert … to service_role` (correct least privilege) *and* a CONTRACT specifying `ON CONFLICT … DO NOTHING` (correct idempotency); Backend wrote the call site against that contract; Sec reviewed the grant as INSERT-only least privilege; and QA asserted that `service_role` **can** INSERT — which went green, because it asserted a *plain* INSERT. Nothing was careless. The seam existed **only between** the artifacts: a targeted `ON CONFLICT` requires `SELECT` on the arbiter columns, so the documented idempotency mechanism would have raised `42501` on **every production run**, not merely on re-runs. It surfaced only when the real statement sequence was executed end-to-end against a live database — something Backend had honestly flagged as never having been done. **Therefore: any surface adopting the W-1 pattern must, before merge, run the actual assembled statement sequence (bind → impersonate → compute → tear down → append) against a live database in a rolled-back transaction, as the deploying identity, using the exact production statement text.** Per-artifact review does not substitute, and a test that exercises an *approximation* of the production statement is worse than no test, because it reports green over a broken path. Sec has landed the battery-level half of this as SECURITY §4.5 **RT-31 leg (h)**; this bullet is the design-time half, recorded here rather than in a code comment because ADR-040 is what the next per-user worker will copy.

**Approved by:** Founder/CTO (2026-08-02) — including the 2026-08-02 Sec-joint-review dispositions folded in above (B1 credential model · B3 D1(d) deferral record · B7 (c′) write-tenant binding fence · the RT-28→RT-31 slot correction · the SD-24 `medium`→`high` tier bump).

**Cross-references.** [ADR-039](#adr-039) (`fn_compute_nav` active-only 2-arg — the worker's read source + the temporal constraint that forces precomputation) · `050` / `019` (`fn_compute_nav` / `fn_holdings_as_of`) · `004` (the Lock 10 mod #8 append-only cross-tier trigger pattern reproduced) · `024` (`user_settings` — the direct `users_id = auth.uid()` RLS precedent) · `025` (the aal2 step-up backstop clause inherited) · [ADR-011](#adr-011) **Decision 1** (privileged-context-write four-clause discipline — (b) satisfied literally under the credential model; **(d) explicitly deferred**, see Consequences) / **Lock 9** (reconciliation/checkpoint precedent) / **Lock 11** (INVOKER read-composition — the worker reuses INVOKER `fn_compute_nav`) / **Lock 12** (`monthly_report` snapshot family — the sibling precomputed-checkpoint shape) / Decision 2 + Decision 14 / Lock 10 (immutable audit-class) / Decision 3 (FK-bypass family — unchanged at 15 labeled / 12 DDL-realized) / Decision 4 (§10 — stays 3) / Decision 9 (DEFINER allowlist — stays 4) · [ADR-023](#adr-023) (**the write role-of-record precedent the ETL credential model adopts** — `authenticator` login + `SET LOCAL ROLE service_role`; `008` least-privilege per-table grants) · [ADR-026](#adr-026) (the reserved general audit-log helper, SELF-201 Task #7 — the D1(d) unblocking dependency) · [ADR-019](#adr-019) (`TenantBoundConnection` at `workers/etl/`) · [ADR-029](#adr-029) (aal2 backstop obligation) · PRD §2.1.2 (net-worth trend) · SECURITY §4.4 (**SD-24**, `high`) / §4.5 (**RT-31**, `high`).
